HIGH⛔ block · RG-001StaticCWE-400CWE-407

1. python-multipart: Denial of Service via crafted form-urlencoded bodies

Problem

The project directly depends on python-multipart version 0.0.20, which is known to contain CVE-2026-53539. This vulnerability allows an attacker to craft malicious form‑urlencoded bodies that trigger a denial‑of‑service condition in the library. The supplied requirements.txt shows the vulnerable version without any mitigating guard or version pinning to a safe release.

Impact

An attacker can cause the application to become unresponsive by sending specially crafted multipart/form‑urlencoded requests, leading to service disruption and potential loss of availability.

Location

requirements.txt

Detected by

  • trivy: CVE-2026-53539

Evidence

  • Dependency vulnerability CVE-2026-53539 in python-multipart 0.0.20; fixed in 0.0.30.
  • requirements.txt lists python-multipart==0.0.20

Recommended fix

Upgrade python-multipart to version 0.0.30 or later, which contains the fix for CVE-2026-53539. Update the requirements.txt accordingly and redeploy the application.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner trivy high at requirements.txt:? rules=CVE-2026-53539

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-051697f9b8567d596874

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001Dynamic

2. A note must belong to the order named in the URL

Problem

The endpoint /orders/{order_id}/notes/{note_id} retrieves a note by its primary key without verifying that the note's order_id matches the order_id supplied in the URL. The code fetches the row based solely on note_id and returns it if found, missing an authorization check that ties the note to the requested order. Runtime probing confirms the flaw: a GET request to /orders/1/notes/2 returns HTTP 200 instead of the expected 403/404, demonstrating that notes from other orders are accessible.

Impact

An attacker can enumerate or retrieve notes belonging to any order by guessing note IDs, leading to unauthorized disclosure of potentially sensitive information and violation of data isolation between orders.

Location

/orders/1/notes/2

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 1

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows no check of order_id against the fetched note's order_id (lines 543‑549).
  • Runtime probe fact reports status 200 for /orders/1/notes/2 where a 403/404 was expected, indicating the missing guard.

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · workflow.nested_note.1 · target /orders/1/notes/2 · HIGHmedium confidence

Steps executed by the workflow runner in this run, with the HTTP status actually observed.

  1. 1. user_a: GET /orders/1/notes/2violation - HTTP 200, expected 403, 404, checks 0/1 passed <- reported step
Recorded output

assertion_resultsfail
expected_statuses403, 404
methodGET
status200
step1
values_redactedyes
workflow_idnested_note
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severityhigh
confidencemedium

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Recommended fix

After fetching the note, compare row['order_id'] with the order_id path parameter. If they differ, raise an HTTPException with status 403 (or 404) to prevent access. Example:
``python
row = conn.execute("SELECT id,order_id,body FROM notes WHERE id=%s", (note_id,)).fetchone()
if not row or row['order_id'] != order_id:
raise HTTPException(404, "Note not found")
return dict(row)
``

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner web-runtime high at /orders/1/notes/2:? rules=1

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-0f6fe31b81367808748c

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001Dynamic

3. Insecure profile access allowing enumeration of other users' data

Problem

The endpoint /profiles/{profile_id} authenticates the request via principal(authorization) but does not verify that the authenticated user is authorized to view the requested profile. The SQL query returns the full user record (including sensitive fields such as email and SSN) for any profile_id supplied. The runtime probe confirms that a request for another user's profile returns HTTP 200, indicating the guard is missing in practice.

Impact

An attacker can enumerate and retrieve personal data (username, email, SSN) of any user by iterating profile IDs, leading to privacy breaches and potential identity theft.

Location

/profiles/{{other_id}}

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 2

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows endpoint definition without ownership check (lines 98‑104).
  • Runtime probe fact reports GET /profiles/{{other_id}} returned status 200 instead of expected 403/404.

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · workflow.profile_owner.2 · target /profiles/{{other_id}} · HIGHmedium confidence

Steps executed by the workflow runner in this run, with the HTTP status actually observed.

  1. 1. user_b: GET /mepassed - HTTP 200, expected 200, checks 1/1 passed
  2. 2. user_a: GET /profiles/{{other_id}}violation - HTTP 200, expected 403, 404, checks 0/1 passed <- reported step
Recorded output

expected_statuses403, 404
methodGET

values_redactedyes
workflow_idprofile_owner
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severityhigh
confidencemedium

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Recommended fix

Add an ownership check after retrieving the row, e.g., compare row.id with the authenticated user's ID and return 403 if they differ. Also limit returned fields to only non‑sensitive data or use a separate DTO. Ensure proper parameterisation of the query to prevent SQL injection.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner web-runtime high at /profiles/{{other_id}}:? rules=2

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-18063ba27bc289f53c8a

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001StaticCWE-606CWE-770

4. python-multipart: Denial of Service via excessive multipart part headers

Problem

The project directly depends on python-multipart version 0.0.20, which is known to contain CVE-2026-42561. This vulnerability allows an attacker to cause a denial‑of‑service condition by sending a multipart request with an excessive number of part headers, exhausting memory or CPU resources. The supplied dependency list shows the vulnerable version is in use, and no mitigation (e.g., version pinning to a safe release) is present in the repository.

Impact

An attacker can craft malicious multipart HTTP requests that trigger the vulnerable parsing logic, potentially crashing the service or degrading performance, leading to denial of service for legitimate users.

Location

requirements.txt

Detected by

  • trivy: CVE-2026-42561

Evidence

  • Dependency vulnerability CVE-2026-42561 in python-multipart 0.0.20; fixed in 0.0.27.
  • requirements.txt lists python-multipart==0.0.20

Recommended fix

Upgrade python-multipart to version 0.0.27 or later, where the issue is fixed. Update the requirements.txt accordingly and run dependency installation again.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner trivy high at requirements.txt:? rules=CVE-2026-42561

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001Dynamic

5. Only the owner can read an invoice

Problem

The endpoint /invoices/{invoice_id} calls principal(authorization) but does not verify that the authenticated user is the owner of the requested invoice before returning the data. The runtime probe for this path returned HTTP 200 instead of the expected 403, confirming that the ownership check is missing and the invoice is disclosed to any authenticated user.

Impact

Unauthorized users can retrieve invoice details of other users, leading to confidentiality breaches and potential privacy violations.

Location

/invoices/{{invoice_id}}

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 3

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows no ownership check after fetching the invoice row (lines 268‑272).
  • Runtime probe fact indicates the request returned status 200 when a 403 was expected (rule workflow.invoice_owner.3).

Issue group merged-54b1ddb055358b3579a2

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001Dynamic

6. Order creation allows setting arbitrary status including paid

Problem

The create_order endpoint directly inserts the order into the database using the status value supplied in the request body without any validation or restriction. The runtime probe shows that a POST to /orders with an arbitrary status returns HTTP 200, whereas the expected behavior is a 400/422 error. This demonstrates that the endpoint accepts and persists a paid status for a newly created order, violating the intended workflow that orders should start as unpaid.

Impact

An attacker can create orders that are immediately marked as paid, potentially bypassing payment processing, causing financial discrepancies, and enabling fraud or unauthorized resource allocation.

Location

/orders

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 1

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows create_order inserts body.status directly into the orders table (lines 213‑215).
  • Runtime probe fact indicates POST /orders returned 200 instead of expected 400/422, confirming the lack of validation.

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · workflow.order_initial_state.1 · target /orders · HIGHmedium confidence

Steps executed by the workflow runner in this run, with the HTTP status actually observed.

  1. 1. user_a: POST /ordersviolation - HTTP 200, expected 400, 422, checks 0/1 passed <- reported step
Recorded output

expected_statuses400, 422
methodPOST
status200
step1
values_redactedyes
workflow_idorder_initial_state
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severityhigh
confidencemedium

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Recommended fix

Validate the status field in the request body, ensuring that only allowed initial states (e.g., "pending" or "unpaid") are accepted. Reject or ignore any attempt to set the status to "paid" or other privileged states during order creation. Implement server‑side checks and return appropriate error codes (400/422) for invalid status values.

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner web-runtime high at /orders:? rules=1

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-5a7c783c30e11426af42

HIGH⛔ block · RG-001Dynamic

7. Webhook destination must match the approved internal host exactly

Problem

The endpoint /webhooks accepts a JSON body containing a target_url. The code only checks that the string "mock-external" is present in the URL (if "mock-external" not in target_url: raise HTTPException(400, ...)). This substring check does not enforce an exact match to an approved internal host and can be bypassed by supplying a URL that includes the required substring but points to an external resource. No additional validation or allow‑list is performed before the URL is stored and later used, providing an open redirect/webhook injection risk.

Impact

An attacker can supply a malicious target_url that passes the weak substring check, causing the application to send data to an arbitrary external endpoint. This can lead to data exfiltration, server‑side request forgery (SSRF), or abuse of the service as a proxy for further attacks.

Location

/webhooks

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 1

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows only a substring check on target_url (lines 295‑298).
  • Runtime probe fact indicates a POST to /webhooks returned HTTP 200 instead of the expected 400/422, demonstrating that the validation was bypassed.

Dynamic test trace

  • No explicit comparison between the authenticated user and the order's owner is present in the supplied snippet.
  • There is no evidence of database triggers, middleware, or other layers that might enforce ownership checks outside the shown function.

Recommended fix

For example, retrieve the user ID from principal(authorization) and compare it to the owner_id field of the order, returning a 403 error if they do not match. 24. Insecure password storage (CWE-256)

Problem

The register endpoint inserts the received password directly into the users table without any hashing or key‑stretching step. The supplied code shows the password value (body.password) being passed unchanged to the SQL INSERT statement, and no surrounding code or middleware is visible that would transform the password before persistence. This pattern strongly suggests that passwords are stored in plaintext, which is a serious security weakness.

Impact

If passwords are stored in plaintext, compromise of the database (through SQL injection, backup leakage, insider threat, or other means) would expose all user credentials directly. Attackers could reuse these credentials on other services, leading to credential stuffing and broader account compromise.

Location

api/app.py:176

Detected by

  • AI discovery (source verified)

Evidence

  • api/app.py:176‑183 – register function inserts body.password directly into the database without hashing

Limitations

  • No evidence is provided that a database trigger, ORM layer, or middleware hashes the password before the INSERT. No scanner or runtime probe confirms the plaintext storage behavior. Additional evidence such as a hash function call, trigger definition, or runtime observation of stored password format is needed to confirm the vulnerability.

Recommended fix

Hash passwords before storing them. Use a strong, adaptive password‑hashing algorithm such as Argon2id, bcrypt, or scrypt with a unique per‑user salt. Replace the raw INSERT with code that hashes body.password first, e.g.,
``python
hashed = argon2.hash(body.password)
conn.execute("INSERT INTO users (username,password,role,email,ssn) VALUES (%s,%s,'user',%s,'000-00-9999')", (body.username, hashed, f"{body.username}@example.test"))
``

Why this decision

  • RG-003: high likely vulnerability requires manual review; not auto-confirmed.
  • source verification confirmed api/app.py:176 (file_exists, line_range_valid, evidence_present_in_excerpt, candidate_context_snapshot_matches)

32. SQL query built from user-controlled sources

Problem

The legacy_login endpoint constructs an SQL query by directly interpolating the user‑provided username and password into a string (f"SELECT … WHERE username='{body.username}' AND password='{body.password}'"). This creates a classic SQL injection vector because the input is not sanitized or parameterized, and no explicit validation or escaping is shown in the provided excerpt. The scanner flagged this as high severity, and the code snippet confirms the risky pattern.

Impact

An attacker could supply specially crafted username or password values to manipulate the SQL query, potentially retrieving unauthorized data, bypassing authentication, or executing arbitrary SQL commands against the database.

Location

api/extra.py:119

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/sql-injection

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows f‑string interpolation of body.username and body.password into the SQL query (lines 117‑119).
  • Scanner finding categorizes this as a high‑severity SQL injection risk.

Limitations

  • No independent corroboration such as runtime probe evidence or a second scanner source confirming exploitability is provided. The review relies solely on the code snippet and the scanner’s classification.

Recommended fix

Rewrite the query to use parameterized statements provided by the database library (e.g., prepared statements or ORM query methods). Example:
``python
query = "SELECT id, username, role FROM users WHERE username = %s AND password = %s"
row = conn.execute(query, (body.username, body.password)).fetchone()
``
Additionally, consider hashing passwords and using secure authentication mechanisms instead of storing plaintext passwords.

Why this decision

  • RG-003: high likely vulnerability requires manual review; not auto-confirmed.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:119 rules=py/sql-injection

40. Reflected server-side cross-site scripting in catalog_search endpoint

Problem

The catalog_search endpoint directly interpolates the user‑controlled query parameter q into an HTML response without any escaping or sanitisation, and also includes database‑derived product names in the same response. This pattern can allow an attacker to inject arbitrary HTML/JavaScript, resulting in reflected XSS. The source excerpt shows the vulnerable code, and an AI‑generated analysis corroborates the issue. However, no independent runtime probe or higher‑severity scanner confirmation is present, and there is no evidence of mitigating headers (e.g., CSP) or additional input validation elsewhere in the codebase.

Impact

An attacker who can trick a victim into visiting a crafted URL (e.g., /catalog/search?q=<script>alert(1)</script>) could execute arbitrary JavaScript in the victim’s browser, leading to session hijacking, credential theft, or other malicious actions.

Location

api/extra.py:145

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/reflective-xss
  • AI discovery (source verified)

Evidence

  • Source excerpt lines 141‑145 show q being interpolated directly into the HTML string returned by the endpoint.
  • AI analysis identifies the same pattern and describes how an attacker could inject <script> payloads.

Limitations

  • No runtime probe evidence demonstrating successful XSS exploitation.
  • No higher‑severity scanner confirmation (severity ≥ medium) beyond the existing scanner finding.
  • No information about response headers (e.g., CSP) or other defensive mechanisms that might mitigate impact.

Recommended fix

Sanitise or HTML‑escape the q parameter and any product names before embedding them in the response. Use a templating engine that auto‑escapes output (e.g., Jinja2) or explicitly apply an escaping function. Consider adding a Content‑Security‑Policy header to further mitigate XSS risk.

Why this decision

  • RG-003: high likely vulnerability requires manual review; not auto-confirmed.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:145 rules=py/reflective-xss

43. SQL query built from user-controlled sources

Problem

The endpoint catalog_search constructs an SQL statement by interpolating the user‑provided query parameter q directly into the query string via an f‑string. This creates a classic SQL injection vector because the input is not sanitized or passed as a parameter to the database driver. No input validation or escaping is shown in the supplied excerpt, and no guard (e.g., ORM parameterization, whitelist, or escaping function) is present in the provided context.

Impact

An attacker could supply crafted input for q that alters the SQL query logic, potentially retrieving unauthorized data, modifying database contents, or executing arbitrary SQL commands.

Location

api/extra.py:144

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/sql-injection

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows conn.execute(f"SELECT name FROM products WHERE name ILIKE '%{q}%'" ) with q coming directly from the request query parameter.
  • Scanner evidence flags this as a high‑severity issue.

Limitations

  • No runtime probe evidence demonstrating successful exploitation.
  • No additional code context confirming the absence of downstream sanitization or ORM usage.

Recommended fix

Refactor the query to use parameterized statements provided by the database library (e.g., conn.execute('SELECT name FROM products WHERE name ILIKE %s', (f"%{q}%",))). Additionally, validate or whitelist allowed characters for q and consider using an ORM that handles query construction safely.

Why this decision

  • RG-003: high likely vulnerability requires manual review; not auto-confirmed.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:144 rules=py/sql-injection

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Issue group merged-51bf51abe866bf8d3d67

67. Content Security Policy (CSP) Header Not Set

Problem

The supplied source excerpt shows the login endpoint implementation but does not include any logic that sets a Content‑Security‑Policy header on the response. The runtime probe reports a POST request to /auth/form-login without indicating that a CSP header was present. No other evidence (e.g., middleware, framework configuration) is provided that would add the header automatically. Consequently, the finding that the CSP header is missing is plausible but not definitively proven by the available artifacts.

Impact

Without a CSP header, the application is vulnerable to client‑side injection attacks such as cross‑site scripting (XSS). An attacker who can inject malicious scripts into pages served by the application could execute arbitrary code in the victim’s browser, potentially stealing session cookies or performing actions on behalf of the user.

Location

/auth/form-login

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 10038

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows no CSP header being set in the form_login handler.
  • Runtime probe fact for POST /auth/form-login does not report a CSP header.

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · zap.10038 · target /auth/form-login · MEDIUMhigh confidence

This scanner does not define a step sequence for this rule, so only the recorded output is shown.

Recorded output
cwe_id693
methodPOST
parameter
scanner_rule10038
values_redactedyes
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severitymedium
confidencehigh

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Limitations

  • Missing evidence of any global middleware, framework settings, or server configuration that might add the CSP header automatically.
  • No runtime observation of response headers confirming the header is absent.

Recommended fix

Configure the application to include a strong Content‑Security‑Policy header on all responses. This can be done by adding middleware that sets the header, e.g., using Starlette/FastAPI middleware:
``python
from starlette.middleware.base import BaseHTTPMiddleware

class CSPMiddleware(BaseHTTPMiddleware):
async def dispatch(self, request, call_next):
response = await call_next(request)
response.headers['Content-Security-Policy'] = "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'"
return response
``
Alternatively, configure the web server (e.g., Nginx, Apache) to add the header globally.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: medium likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner web-runtime medium at /auth/form-login:? rules=10038

Issue group merged-6e468e09eab3ca889e3d

MEDIUM⚠️ warn · RG-006DynamicCWE-693

69. Content Security Policy (CSP) Header Not Set

Problem

The endpoint /comments returns HTML generated from comment bodies without setting a Content‑Security‑Policy header. The source excerpt shows the response is built as a plain string and returned via FastAPI's HTMLResponse default, which does not include CSP headers. A runtime probe (zap.10038) observed a GET request to /comments, confirming the endpoint is reachable, but did not capture response headers, so the absence of CSP cannot be directly proven at runtime.

Impact

Without a CSP header, browsers are not instructed to restrict the sources from which scripts, styles, or other resources may be loaded. An attacker who can inject malicious content into comment bodies could execute arbitrary JavaScript in a victim's browser (XSS), leading to session hijacking, data exfiltration, or further compromise.

Location

/comments

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 10038

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows /comments endpoint returns HTML without setting headers
  • Runtime probe fact (zap.10038) confirms the endpoint is reachable via GET

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · zap.10038 · target /comments · MEDIUMhigh confidence

This scanner does not define a step sequence for this rule, so only the recorded output is shown.

Recorded output
cwe_id693
methodGET
parameter
scanner_rule10038
values_redactedyes
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severitymedium
confidencehigh

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Limitations

  • No captured response headers from the runtime probe to definitively prove the CSP header is missing
  • No evidence that the application sets CSP globally via middleware elsewhere in the codebase

Recommended fix

Add a CSP header to the response. In FastAPI this can be done with a middleware or by customizing the response, e.g.:``python
from fastapi.responses import HTMLResponse
from starlette.middleware.base import BaseHTTPMiddleware

class CSPMiddleware(BaseHTTPMiddleware):
async def dispatch(self, request, call_next):
response = await call_next(request)
response.headers["Content‑Security‑Policy"] = "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'"
return response

app.add_middleware(CSPMiddleware)
`
Alternatively, set the header directly in the endpoint:`python
return HTMLResponse(content=html, headers={"Content‑Security‑Policy": "default-src 'self'"})``

Why this decision

  • RG-006: medium likely vulnerability is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner web-runtime medium at /comments:? rules=10038

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

Proven Missing authorization check on webhook creation (CWE-284) · 3 attempt(s)

unauthenticated_access: request to /webhooks as anonymous returned HTTP 405, not one of the expected denial statuses [401, 403, 404].

2. A note must belong to the order named in the URL

/orders/1/notes/2

Problem

The endpoint /orders/{order_id}/notes/{note_id} retrieves a note by its primary key without verifying that the note's order_id matches the order_id supplied in the URL. The code fetches the row based solely on note_id and returns it if found, missing an authorization check that ties the note to the requested order. Runtime probing confirms the flaw: a GET request to /orders/1/notes/2 returns HTTP 200 instead of the expected 403/404, demonstrating that notes from other orders are accessible.

Impact

An attacker can enumerate or retrieve notes belonging to any order by guessing note IDs, leading to unauthorized disclosure of potentially sensitive information and violation of data isolation between orders.

Location

/orders/1/notes/2

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 1

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows no check of order_id against the fetched note's order_id (lines 543‑549).
  • Runtime probe fact reports status 200 for /orders/1/notes/2 where a 403/404 was expected, indicating the missing guard.

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · workflow.nested_note.1 · target /orders/1/notes/2 · HIGHmedium confidence

Steps executed by the workflow runner in this run, with the HTTP status actually observed.

  1. 1. user_a: GET /orders/1/notes/2violation - HTTP 200, expected 403, 404, checks 0/1 passed <- reported step

vulnerableyes

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Recommended fix

After fetching the note, compare row['order_id'] with the order_id path parameter. If they differ, raise an HTTPException with status 403 (or 404) to prevent access. Example:
``python
row = conn.execute("SELECT id,order_id,body FROM notes WHERE id=%s", (note_id,)).fetchone()
if not row or row['order_id'] != order_id:
raise HTTPException(404, "Note not found")
return dict(row)
``

Why this decision

  • RG-001: high confirmed vulnerability is corroborated by a high scanner finding; blocking severities are critical, high.
  • scanner web-runtime high at /orders/1/notes/2:? rules=1

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

7. Webhook destination must match the approved internal host exactly

/webhooks

Problem

The endpoint /webhooks accepts a JSON body containing a target_url. The code only checks that the string "mock-external" is present in the URL (if "mock-external" not in target_url: raise HTTPException(400, ...)). This substring check does not enforce an exact match to an approved internal host and can be bypassed by supplying a URL that includes the required substring but points to an external resource. No additional validation or allow‑list is performed before the URL is stored and later used, providing an open redirect/webhook injection risk.

Impact

An attacker can supply a malicious target_url that passes the weak substring check, causing the application to send data to an arbitrary external endpoint. This can lead to data exfiltration, server‑side request forgery (SSRF), or abuse of the service as a proxy for further attacks.

Location

/webhooks

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 1

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows only a substring check on target_url (lines 295‑298).
  • Runtime probe fact indicates a POST to /webhooks returned HTTP 200 instead of the expected 400/422, demonstrating that the validation was bypassed.

24. Insecure password storage (CWE-256)

api/app.py:176

Problem

The register endpoint inserts the received password directly into the users table without any hashing or key‑stretching step. The supplied code shows the password value (body.password) being passed unchanged to the SQL INSERT statement, and no surrounding code or middleware is visible that would transform the password before persistence. This pattern strongly suggests that passwords are stored in plaintext, which is a serious security weakness.

Impact

If passwords are stored in plaintext, compromise of the database (through SQL injection, backup leakage, insider threat, or other means) would expose all user credentials directly. Attackers could reuse these credentials on other services, leading to credential stuffing and broader account compromise.

Location

api/app.py:176

Detected by

  • AI discovery (source verified)

Evidence

  • api/app.py:176‑183 – register function inserts body.password directly into the database without hashing

Limitations

  • No evidence is provided that a database trigger, ORM layer, or middleware hashes the password before the INSERT. No scanner or runtime probe confirms the plaintext storage behavior. Additional evidence such as a hash function call, trigger definition, or runtime observation of stored password format is needed to confirm the vulnerability.

Recommended fix

Hash passwords before storing them. Use a strong, adaptive password‑hashing algorithm such as Argon2id, bcrypt, or scrypt with a unique per‑user salt. Replace the raw INSERT with code that hashes body.password first, e.g.,
``python
hashed = argon2.hash(body.password)
conn.execute("INSERT INTO users (username,password,role,email,ssn) VALUES (%s,%s,'user',%s,'000-00-9999')", (body.username, hashed, f"{body.username}@example.test"))
``

Why this decision

  • RG-003: high likely vulnerability requires manual review; not auto-confirmed.
  • source verification confirmed api/app.py:176 (file_exists, line_range_valid, evidence_present_in_excerpt, candidate_context_snapshot_matches)

This allows a caller to set an arbitrary discount percentage, potentially overriding the coupon’s configured discount or applying a discount when no valid coupon exists.

Impact

An attacker could obtain full or excessive discounts on orders, leading to revenue loss and potential abuse of promotional mechanisms.

Location

api/app.py:230

Detected by

  • AI discovery (source verified)

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows percent = body.discount if body.discount is not None else (coupon["percent"] if coupon else 0) (line 235).
  • Sensitive operation UPDATE orders SET discount=%s WHERE id=%s uses the unchecked percent (line 236).

Limitations

  • Missing definition of CouponBody and any constraints on its discount field.
  • No evidence of downstream logic that caps or sanitizes the discount value.
  • No runtime probe or scanner evidence confirming that arbitrary discounts can be applied in practice.

Recommended fix

Validate body.discount against allowed ranges (e.g., 0‑100) and ensure it matches the discount defined for the supplied coupon. If body.discount is omitted, fall back to the coupon’s percent only when a valid coupon is found. Add explicit checks to reject out‑of‑range or unauthorized discount values before executing the UPDATE.

Why this decision

30. Missing ownership check on order cancellation (CWE-639)

api/app.py:258

Problem

The endpoint authenticates the request via principal(authorization) but does not verify that the authenticated user owns the order identified by order_id before executing an UPDATE that cancels the order. Impact

  • No evidence is provided that a database trigger, foreign key constraint, or other backend guard enforces ownership.
  • No independent scanner or runtime probe evidence confirming that the endpoint can be abused without ownership verification.

Recommended fix

Add an ownership verification step before the UPDATE, e.g., query the order's owner_id and compare it to the authenticated principal's user identifier. Abort with an authorization error if they do not match. Optionally enforce ownership at the database level with row‑level security or triggers.

32. SQL query built from user-controlled sources

api/extra.py:119

Problem

The legacy_login endpoint constructs an SQL query by directly interpolating the user‑provided username and password into a string (f"SELECT … WHERE username='{body.username}' AND password='{body.password}'"). This creates a classic SQL injection vector because the input is not sanitized or parameterized, and no explicit validation or escaping is shown in the provided excerpt. The scanner flagged this as high severity, and the code snippet confirms the risky pattern.

Impact

An attacker could supply specially crafted username or password values to manipulate the SQL query, potentially retrieving unauthorized data, bypassing authentication, or executing arbitrary SQL commands against the database.

Location

api/extra.py:119

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/sql-injection

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows f‑string interpolation of body.username and body.password into the SQL query (lines 117‑119).
  • Scanner finding categorizes this as a high‑severity SQL injection risk.

Limitations

  • No independent corroboration such as runtime probe evidence or a second scanner source confirming exploitability is provided. The review relies solely on the code snippet and the scanner’s classification.

Recommended fix

Rewrite the query to use parameterized statements provided by the database library (e.g., prepared statements or ORM query methods). Example:
``python
query = "SELECT id, username, role FROM users WHERE username = %s AND password = %s"
row = conn.execute(query, (body.username, body.password)).fetchone()
``
Additionally, consider hashing passwords and using secure authentication mechanisms instead of storing plaintext passwords.

Why this decision

  • RG-003: high likely vulnerability requires manual review; not auto-confirmed.
  • scanner codeql-cli high at api/extra.py:119 rules=py/sql-injection

Evidence basis: scanner corroborated

40. Reflected server-side cross-site scripting in catalog_search endpoint

api/extra.py:145

Problem

The catalog_search endpoint directly interpolates the user‑controlled query parameter q into an HTML response without any escaping or sanitisation, and also includes database‑derived product names in the same response. This pattern can allow an attacker to inject arbitrary HTML/JavaScript, resulting in reflected XSS. The source excerpt shows the vulnerable code, and an AI‑generated analysis corroborates the issue. However, no independent runtime probe or higher‑severity scanner confirmation is present, and there is no evidence of mitigating headers (e.g., CSP) or additional input validation elsewhere in the codebase.

Impact

An attacker who can trick a victim into visiting a crafted URL (e.g., /catalog/search?q=<script>alert(1)</script>) could execute arbitrary JavaScript in the victim’s browser, leading to session hijacking, credential theft, or other malicious actions.

api/extra.py:145

  • AI discovery (source verified)

Evidence

  • Source excerpt lines 141‑145 show q being interpolated directly into the HTML string returned by the endpoint.
  • AI analysis identifies the same pattern and describes how an attacker could inject <script> payloads.

Limitations

  • No runtime probe evidence demonstrating successful XSS exploitation.
  • No higher‑severity scanner confirmation (severity ≥ medium) beyond the existing scanner finding.
  • No information about response headers (e.g., CSP) or other defensive mechanisms that might mitigate impact.

Recommended fix

Sanitise or HTML‑escape the q parameter and any product names before embedding them in the response. Use a templating engine that auto‑escapes output (e.g., Jinja2) or explicitly apply an escaping function. Consider adding a Content‑Security‑Policy header to further mitigate XSS risk.

43. SQL query built from user-controlled sources

api/extra.py:144

Problem

The endpoint catalog_search constructs an SQL statement by interpolating the user‑provided query parameter q directly into the query string via an f‑string. This creates a classic SQL injection vector because the input is not sanitized or passed as a parameter to the database driver. No input validation or escaping is shown in the supplied excerpt, and no guard (e.g., ORM parameterization, whitelist, or escaping function) is present in the provided context.

Impact

An attacker could supply crafted input for q that alters the SQL query logic, potentially retrieving unauthorized data, modifying database contents, or executing arbitrary SQL commands.

Location

api/extra.py:144

Detected by

  • codeql-cli: py/sql-injection

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows conn.execute(f"SELECT name FROM products WHERE name ILIKE '%{q}%'" ) with q coming directly from the request query parameter.
  • Scanner evidence flags this as a high‑severity issue.

Limitations

  • No runtime probe evidence demonstrating successful exploitation.
  • No additional code context confirming the absence of downstream sanitization or ORM usage.

Recommended fix

Refactor the query to use parameterized statements provided by the database library (e.g., conn.execute('SELECT name FROM products WHERE name ILIKE %s', (f"%{q}%",))). Additionally, validate or whitelist allowed characters for q and consider using an ORM that handles query construction safely.

Why this decision

59. Potential path traversal via unverified file name (CWE-22)

api/extra.py:210

Problem

The function constructs a file path from user‑provided "name" and checks that the resolved path is within the public directory using Path.is_relative_to. While this check can prevent simple "../" traversals on recent Python versions, the code does not address symlink resolution or older Python versions where is_relative_to may be unavailable. If an attacker can place a symlink inside the public directory that points outside, the check could be bypassed, leading to arbitrary file reads.

Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary files on the server, potentially exposing source code, configuration files, or other sensitive data.

Location

api/extra.py:210

Detected by

  • AI discovery (source verified)

  • 211: def file_read(name: str) -> Response:
  • 212: file = (FILES / "public" / name).resolve()

63. Missing rate limiting / token reuse protection on password reset request (CWE-640)

api/extra.py:564

Problem

The code defines a POST endpoint "/auth/reset/request" that generates a reset token and stores it in the database, then returns the token in the response. No logic for rate‑limiting requests per user or IP, nor any token expiration handling, is visible in the supplied excerpts. Without such checks, an attacker could repeatedly invoke the endpoint to obtain many valid reset tokens for a target account, potentially enabling token enumeration or reuse attacks.

Impact

An attacker can flood the password‑reset endpoint to obtain unlimited valid reset tokens for a victim, leading to account takeover if the token is later used to reset the password. Lack of rate limiting also facilitates denial‑of‑service against the reset service.

Location

Recommended fix

67. Content Security Policy (CSP) Header Not Set

/auth/form-login

Problem

The supplied source excerpt shows the login endpoint implementation but does not include any logic that sets a Content‑Security‑Policy header on the response. The runtime probe reports a POST request to /auth/form-login without indicating that a CSP header was present. No other evidence (e.g., middleware, framework configuration) is provided that would add the header automatically. Consequently, the finding that the CSP header is missing is plausible but not definitively proven by the available artifacts.

Impact

Without a CSP header, the application is vulnerable to client‑side injection attacks such as cross‑site scripting (XSS). An attacker who can inject malicious scripts into pages served by the application could execute arbitrary code in the victim’s browser, potentially stealing session cookies or performing actions on behalf of the user.

Location

/auth/form-login

Detected by

  • web-runtime: 10038

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows no CSP header being set in the form_login handler.
  • Runtime probe fact for POST /auth/form-login does not report a CSP header.

Dynamic test trace

Bounded Web Runtime Checks · zap.10038 · target /auth/form-login · MEDIUMhigh confidence

This scanner does not define a step sequence for this rule, so only the recorded output is shown.

Recorded output
cwe_id693
methodPOST
parameter
scanner_rule10038
values_redactedyes
Probe result
vulnerableyes
severitymedium
confidencehigh

The dynamic scanners keep status codes and flags only; response bodies, cookies and credentials are withheld by design.

Limitations

  • Missing evidence of any global middleware, framework settings, or server configuration that might add the CSP header automatically.
  • No runtime observation of response headers confirming the header is absent.

Recommended fix

Configure the application to include a strong Content‑Security‑Policy header on all responses. This can be done by adding middleware that sets the header, e.g., using Starlette/FastAPI middleware:
``python
from starlette.middleware.base import BaseHTTPMiddleware

class CSPMiddleware(BaseHTTPMiddleware):
async def dispatch(self, request, call_next):
response = await call_next(request)
response.headers['Content-Security-Policy'] = "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'"
return response
``
Alternatively, configure the web server (e.g., Nginx, Apache) to add the header globally.

77. Information leakage via detailed error messages (CWE-209)

api/extra.py:227

Problem

Replace the detailed exception message with a generic error response. Issue group merged-19f4a13e0b2d4f7413d0

LOW⚠️ warn · RG-006StaticCWE-939

91. Dynamic Urllib Use Detected

tests/test_smoke.py:20-22

Problem

The code constructs a URL by concatenating a constant BASE with a variable path argument and then opens it with urllib.request.urlopen. While urllib can handle file:// URLs, the supplied source excerpt does not show that the path argument is derived from untrusted user input, nor does it demonstrate a missing validation check. No runtime probe facts or additional scanner evidence confirm that an attacker can control the URL to trigger an unsafe file read.

Impact

If an attacker could control the path value and the application allowed file:// schemes, they might read arbitrary files from the server, leading to information disclosure.

Location

tests/test_smoke.py:20-22

Detected by

  • semgrep-ce: dynamic-urllib-use-detected

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows urllib.request.urlopen called with BASE + path (lines 20‑22).

Limitations

  • Missing evidence that path originates from untrusted input; no guard or validation shown in the provided context. No runtime probe demonstrating exploitation of a file:// scheme. No additional scanner findings of medium or higher severity confirming the vulnerability.

Recommended fix

Validate and sanitize the path argument to ensure it does not contain unsafe schemes (e.g., file://) or directory traversal. Consider using a higher‑level HTTP client like the requests library, which does not support file URLs, or explicitly reject non‑HTTP schemes before calling urllib.

Why this decision

  • RG-006: low needs more evidence is not sufficiently confirmed for an automatic block.
  • scanner semgrep-ce low at tests/test_smoke.py:20 rules=dynamic-urllib-use-detected

Evidence basis: ai assertion only

92. Dynamic Urllib Use Detected

worker/worker.py:23

Problem

The code constructs a urllib request using a value from the incoming payload (payload["target_url"]). No validation or sanitisation of this URL is shown, and no guard (e.g., scheme whitelist) is present in the supplied snippet. However, the finding lacks independent corroboration: there is no additional scanner of medium/higher severity, no runtime probe demonstrating that an attacker‑controlled URL can be supplied, and no evidence that the URL is later constrained. Consequently the evidence is insufficient to confirm a vulnerability.

Impact

If an attacker can influence the "target_url" field, they may cause the worker to fetch arbitrary resources, potentially leading to SSRF or unintended file reads via file:// schemes.

Location

worker/worker.py:23

Detected by

  • semgrep-ce: dynamic-urllib-use-detected

Evidence

  • Source excerpt shows urllib.request.urlopen called with payload["target_url"] without validation.

Limitations

  • Missing runtime probe that demonstrates attacker‑controlled input reaching urllib, no additional scanner evidence of medium or higher severity, and no view of surrounding code that might perform URL validation elsewhere.

Recommended fix

Validate the "target_url" value before using it with urllib. Restrict allowed schemes (e.g., only http/https), enforce a whitelist of domains, or switch to a safer HTTP client like the requests library that does not support file:// URLs by default.

LOW✅ allow · RG-004Static

95. No HEALTHCHECK defined

Dockerfile.mock

Problem

The finding reports the absence of a HEALTHCHECK instruction in the Dockerfile. While adding a HEALTHCHECK is a recommended best practice for container health monitoring, its absence does not constitute a security vulnerability or a misconfiguration that directly leads to an exploitable condition. No evidence is provided that the missing HEALTHCHECK results in insecure behavior, privilege escalation, or denial of service. Therefore, this is not a security flaw.

Impact

None – the container will run without health monitoring, which may affect operational reliability but does not create a security risk.

Location

Dockerfile.mock

Detected by

  • trivy: DS-0026

Evidence

  • Dockerfile excerpt shows no HEALTHCHECK line

Recommended fix

Add a HEALTHCHECK instruction to the Dockerfile to enable Docker to monitor container health, e.g.,

HEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=5s --retries=3 CMD curl -f http://localhost:9000/health || exit 1

Why this decision

  • RG-004: classified as a false positive; no high/critical scanner finding contradicts it.
  • scanner trivy low at Dockerfile.mock:? rules=DS-0026

Evidence basis: ai assertion only

Known gaps (8)

Each gap lowers confidence in a clean result for that area.

  • A negative result means only that this probe did not observe the behavior.
  • A violation is an observed contract mismatch requiring security review.
  • Baseline checks use bounded HTTP GET observations for an authorized target; declared paths must be safe to visit.
  • Browser crawl is same-origin and GET-only; forms, clicks, OAuth/MFA and destructive workflows were not submitted.
  • Coverage applies only to declared assertions, accounts and workflows.
  • Fresh stack resets the run; workflows must create independent test data. No per-step database reset is performed.
  • Resolved transitive dependency versions are unverified for component component-cdb4ee2aea: a manifest was found, but no supported lockfile. All 4 direct dependencies are exactly pinned; their transitive dependencies are not recorded.
  • This baseline phase only makes bounded HTTP GET observations; browser crawling, ZAP and account workflows are separate phases, reported only when they ran.

How to read this report

Observation

A raw scanner or DAST signal before review.

Issue group & verdict

Correlated evidence assessed by the Review Agent. A model assessment, not independent exploit proof.

Found by

Static = scanners reading code, dependencies, secrets and configuration. Dynamic = probes sent to the running app. AI discovery = candidates the AI agent found by reading source.

Policy decision

A release-gate outcome from a named rule. Never add it to the vulnerability count.

Coverage gap

No finding in an untested area does not mean it passed.
